WhatsApp’s AI-powered photo expansion tool starts rolling out on iOS

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Select WhatsApp users on TestFlight and in the main app are now seeing the option to expand an image in the app’s photo editor. Here are the details.

iPhone users with access to Apple Intelligence have a native AI-powered photo expansion tool called Extend, which lets users pinch or drag to expand an image beyond its original borders.

This feature, which is also present on AI chatbots such as ChatGPT and image-editing platforms like Photoshop, is now also making its way into WhatsApp.

According to WABetaInfo, the platform is rolling out a Meta AI-powered image expansion tool, though it is currently limited to select users, primarily those in WhatsApp’s beta program via TestFlight.

When users select a photo in their chats, WhatsApp opens the drawing editor. Here, they can choose to apply some AI effect to improve it. Among these, users can find a new option to expand their photos. Once users select this option, Meta AI takes a few seconds to analyze the photo and generate the expanded version. Once the process is complete, WhatsApp shows the expanded photo in the drawing editor, and users can choose to send it. Note that this feature is also compatible with status updates.

The site notes that if the user isn’t happy with the result, they can tap an undo button to revert to the original and then try using the tool again to generate a different result.

As is usually the case with beta features on WhatsApp, there is no telling when it might become widely available.

That said, given that it has also rolled out to users in the Android beta and some users on the public iOS version already have access, a broader rollout might not be too far off.

These Panera Snowman Cups Are Only Available for a Day—Here’s How to Get One

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Panera Snowman Cup is giving the Starbucks Bearista Cup some cozy competition. Here's what you need to know about the limited-edition mystery boxes.

I don’t generally need an incentive to stop at Panera in December. Any of those delicious soups in a bread bowl is a fairly convincing argument on its own. But this year, the chain is giving us another reason to visit: collectible glass snowman cups, with a chance to get one free.

If you’ve been admiring the Starbucks Bearista cup, these little snowmen might also deserve a spot on your wish list. Panera’s version comes in a mystery box containing one of two designs, naughty or nice. The free cup offer runs on Dec. 3, and with only 12 available at each participating cafe, there are a few details to keep in mind before you go.

The limited-edition glass cups come in two designs inspired by the naughty-and-nice theme of Panera’s holiday packaging. Each mystery box holds one snowman cup, so you won’t know which you’ve received until you open it.

There are also a few new things to drink while you admire your cute cup. Panera’s new holiday menu, rolling out this week, includes peppermint white mocha and pistachio white mocha beverages. Both flavors are available as a latte, iced coffee or Frozen Java, finished with peppermint bark pieces or pistachio pieces. The iced coffees have sweet cream cold foam, and the lattes and Frozen Java drinks get whipped cream.

On Dec. 3, the first 12 customers at each participating U.S. cafe who make a qualifying purchase of $25 or more will receive a free Snowman Cup, while supplies last. There’s a limit of one per person, so you may want to make that Panera outing a breakfast date.

You’ll need to order inside the cafe at the cashier. Kiosk, Rapid Pick-Up, drive-thru, catering and delivery orders—including those placed through third-party sites—don’t qualify.

If you don’t get your hands on one of those first 12 boxes, you’ll have another way to find a snowman. A limited number of cups will also be available through the Panera Shop for $22 each. An online sale date hasn’t been announced yet, so keep an eye on the shop if you’re hoping to grab one.

Threat actor targets Indian fintechs on weekends when defenders aren’t watching

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CERT-IN warns of ‘significant escalation’ in AI-powered attacks, some after victims tried to improve defences

India’s Computer Emergency Response Team has warned the nation’s financial services industry of a “significant escalation” in attacks by what it describes as an “AI-assisted financial threat actor” that has re-targeted victims after they flubbed remediation efforts.

CERT-IN first detected the attacks in October and issued advisory in which it warned digital lending platforms, fintech entities, payment service providers, and wallet operators that attackers were targeting internet-facing applications, exposed APIs and privileged endpoints. If the attackers found a way in, they tried to use payment APIs to steal money.

On Thursday, the CERT published an update in which it said it continues to hear of successful attacks, including from outfits that tried to remediate their systems after earlier incidents.

“The same entities have been deliberately re-targeted, including after incomplete remediation and attacks have been timed to coincide with holidays, weekends and other low-monitoring windows to delay detection and response,” the CERT warned.

CERT-IN has decided the attackers need a name and chose “CRAFTY FALCON.”

The security org has also revealed that the crew uses AI-enhanced tools including the ARTEX pen-testing tool, the Cairn attack search engine, and the CyberStrikeAI framework – all of which have been linked to users who appear to speak Chinese.

CRAFTY FALCON’s tactics see it go looking for internet-exposed environments used for testing or development, where it finds artefacts such as shared credentials, tokens, or network paths that help it move into production systems.

If it gets in, the crew re-writes victims’ applications so they do things like make unauthorized API calls. Victims’ defenses assume that only legitimate apps would call those APIs, so activity conducted by the re-written apps aren’t flagged as an attack.

Even if victims suspect an attack, they have a lot to cope with because CERT-IN says CRAFTY FALCON creates many “functionally identical variants refined in rapid succession; a pattern consistent with AI-generated code and iterative AI-assisted development.”

The crew also quickly exploits newly discovered vulnerabilities to keep its attacks fresh.

Once it gets a toehold, the criminal crew makes tiny payments of about $0.01 to test if its attacks work. If those transfers work, CRAFTY FALCON makes larger payments. When the target is a cryptocurrency exchange or virtual digital asset service provider, the crew steals wallet keys and empties cryptocurrency wallets.

Vendors of observability tools will be warming up their Indian sales teams today, because CERT-IN’s advice to Indian businesses includes improving their ability to log events, monitor for anomalies in real time, and send alerts. It also urged keeping test environments off the internet, and restricting access to cloud resources because CRAFTY FALCON is fond of using them to run its attacks.

CERT-IN also warns that the attacks are “active and escalating” and therefore worthy of 24x7 monitoring.

Interestingly, the CERT’s advisory mentions attacks on “entities” across banking, financial services, and insurance, but only names the kind of businesses mentioned above as having reported attacks.

The Register points this out because India is home to literally thousands of banks, many of them small operations that sometimes lack basic infosec protections. CRAFTY FALCON therefore has a target-rich environment in India should it choose to expand its activities. ®

Taiwan’s Currency Weakens Past 33 Per US Dollar on AI Jitters

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Taiwan dollar weakened past the key psychological level of 33 per US dollar for the first time since April 2025, as a broad retreat in artificial intelligence shares weighed on the island’s tech-heavy equity market.
